\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{y}\Big[\big(f_{y}(\rho^{(i)};X)-f_{y}(\rho;X)\big)^{2}\big|\big|s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)\big|\big|_{2}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4m}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{y}\Big[\big(f_{y}(\rho^{(i)};X)-f_{y}(\rho;X)\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{4m}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{y}\Bigg[\Bigg(f_{y}(\rho;X)\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\big\langle s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)-\mathbb{E}_{y}^{\rho}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}],\rho^{(i)}-\rho\big\rangle+\frac{1}{2}(\rho^{(i)}-\rho)^{\prime}\frac{\delta^{2}}{\delta{{\rho}^{2}}}f_{y}(\bar{\rho}_{X,y};X)(\rho^{(i)}-\rho)\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8m}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{y}\Bigg[f_{y}(\rho;X)^{2}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{(\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}})^{2}}||s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)-\mathbb{E}_{y}^{\rho}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]||_{2}^{2}||\rho^{(i)}-\rho||_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{\textup{max}}\Bigg\{\frac{\delta^{2}}{\delta{{\rho}^{2}}}f_{y}(\bar{\rho}_{X,y};X)\Bigg\}^{2}||\rho^{(i)}-\rho||_{2}^{4}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8m}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{y}\Bigg[\frac{16m}{L^{2}\xi^{2}}f_{y}(\rho;X)^{2}||\rho^{(i)}-\rho||_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{\textup{max}}\Bigg\{\frac{\delta^{2}}{\delta{{\rho}^{2}}}f_{y}(\bar{\rho}_{X,y};X)\Bigg\}^{2}||\rho^{(i)}-\rho||_{2}^{4}\Bigg],

which, by our earlier arguments, converges to
0
as
\theta^{(i)}\to\theta
. In addition, note that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{y}^{\rho}\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{q_{y}^{\rho^{(i)}}(x)^{1/2}-q_{y}^{\rho}(x)^{1/2}}{q_{y}^{\rho}(x)^{1/2}}\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{y}^{\rho}\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{q_{y}^{\rho^{(i)}}(x)-q_{y}^{\rho}(x)}{q_{y}^{\rho}(x)^{1/2}\big(q_{y}^{\rho^{(i)}}(x)^{1/2}+q_{y}^{\rho}(x)^{1/2}\big)}\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{y}^{\rho}\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{q_{y}^{\rho^{(i)}}(x)-q_{y}^{\rho}(x)}{q_{y}^{\rho}(x)}\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg].

By line (
48
), the RHS converges to
0
as
\theta^{(i)}\to\theta
. Thus, overall, we have that
\int(1-d)W_{3}(z,\theta,\theta^{(i)})\ d\widetilde{\mu}(z)\to 0
as
\theta^{(i)}\to\theta
.
And, having shown that all three integrals (involving
W_{1},W_{2}
and
W_{3}
) converge to
0
, we may conclude that
\int||D(z,\theta^{(i)})-D(z,\theta)||_{2}^{2}d\widetilde{\mu}(z)\to 0
. Thus, we have verified property (b)!

Finally, let’s prove property (a)
. Observe that:

\displaystyle\int||D(z,\theta)||_{2}^{2}d\widetilde{\mu}(z)

\displaystyle=\int||D(z,\pi;\rho)||_{2}^{2}d\widetilde{\mu}(z)+\int||D(z,\rho;\pi)||_{2}^{2}d\widetilde{\mu}(z)

\displaystyle\leq\int dq_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)||s_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)||_{2}^{2}d\widetilde{\mu}(z)+\int||D(z,\rho;\pi)||_{2}^{2}d\widetilde{\mu}(z)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4m}{\xi^{2}}+\int||D(z,\rho;\pi)||_{2}^{2}d\widetilde{\mu}(z).

Now, note that:

\displaystyle||D(z,\rho;\pi)||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq d\sqrt{\tau}\frac{1}{\xi}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}\frac{q_{j}^{\rho}(x)}{2\sqrt{q_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)}}\big|\big|s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{j}^{\rho}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big|\big|_{2}+(1-d)\sqrt{(1-\tau)\pi_{y}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{q_{y}^{\rho}(x)}\frac{1}{\xi}||s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{y}^{\rho}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq d\frac{1}{\xi}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{q_{j}^{\rho}(x)}{\sqrt{q_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)}}\big|\big|s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{j}^{\rho}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big|\big|_{2}+(1-d)\sqrt{q_{y}^{\rho}(x)}\frac{1}{\xi}||s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{y}^{\rho}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq d\frac{4\sqrt{m}}{L\xi}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{q_{j}^{\rho}(x)}{\sqrt{q_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)}}+(1-d)\frac{4\sqrt{m}}{L\xi}\sqrt{q_{y}^{\rho}(x)}

\displaystyle\leq d\frac{4\sqrt{m}}{L\xi}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{q_{j}^{\rho}(x)}{\sqrt{\pi_{j}q_{j}^{\rho}(x)}}+(1-d)\frac{4\sqrt{m}}{L\xi}\sqrt{q_{y}^{\rho}(x)}

\displaystyle=d\frac{4\sqrt{m}}{L\xi^{3/2}}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\sqrt{q_{j}^{\rho}(x)}+(1-d)\frac{4\sqrt{m}}{L\xi}\sqrt{q_{y}^{\rho}(x)}.

Thus:

\displaystyle||D(z,\rho;\pi)||_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq d\frac{32m}{L^{2}\xi^{3}}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}\sqrt{q_{j}^{\rho}(x)}\bigg)^{2}+(1-d)\frac{32m}{L^{2}\xi^{2}}q_{y}^{\rho}(x)

\displaystyle\leq d\frac{32m^{2}}{L^{2}\xi^{3}}\sum_{j=0}^{m}q_{j}^{\rho}(x)+(1-d)\frac{32m}{L^{2}\xi^{2}}q_{y}^{\rho}(x).

Ergo:

\displaystyle\int||D(z,\rho;\pi)||_{2}^{2}d\widetilde{\mu}(z)

\displaystyle\leq\int\bigg[d\frac{32m^{2}}{L^{2}\xi^{3}}\sum_{j=0}^{m}q_{j}^{\rho}(X)+(1-d)\frac{32m}{L^{2}\xi^{2}}q_{y}^{\rho}(X)\bigg]d\widetilde{\mu}(z)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{32m^{3}}{L^{2}\xi^{3}}+\frac{32m^{2}}{L^{2}\xi^{2}}.

Thus, overall,

\displaystyle\int||D(z,\theta)||_{2}^{2}d\widetilde{\mu}(z)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4m}{\xi^{2}}+\frac{32m^{3}}{L^{2}\xi^{3}}+\frac{32m^{2}}{L^{2}\xi^{2}}

\displaystyle<\infty,

meaning that property (a) is true!

We have therefore verified properties (a), (b) and (c). Finally, it remains for us to prove the score function identities in the statement of this lemma. By Definition A.1 of
[
Newey, 1990
]
, the score function
S_{\pi,\rho}^{\tau}
for the parameters
(\pi,\rho)
is given by:

\displaystyle S_{\pi,\rho}^{\tau}(z)

\displaystyle=2\frac{D(z,\pi,\rho)}{\mathcal{J}^{\pi,\rho,\tau}(z)^{1/2}}\mathbbold{1}{\big\{\mathcal{J}^{\pi,\rho,\tau}(z)>0\big\}}

\displaystyle=2\frac{\begin{pmatrix}D(z,\pi;\rho)\\
D(z,\rho;\pi)\end{pmatrix}}{\mathcal{J}^{\pi,\rho,\tau}(z)^{1/2}}\mathbbold{1}{\big\{\mathcal{J}^{\pi,\rho,\tau}(z)>0\big\}}.

Note that:

\displaystyle 2\frac{D(z,\pi;\rho)}{\mathcal{J}^{\pi,\rho,\tau}(z)^{1/2}}\mathbbold{1}{\big\{\mathcal{J}^{\pi,\rho,\tau}(z)>0\big\}}

\displaystyle=2\frac{\frac{d}{2}\sqrt{\tau q_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)}s_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)}{\mathcal{J}^{\pi,\rho,\tau}(z)^{1/2}}\mathbbold{1}{\big\{\mathcal{J}^{\pi,\rho,\tau}(z)>0\big\}}

\displaystyle=\frac{d\sqrt{\tau q_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)}s_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)}{\sqrt{\tau q_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)}}\mathbbold{1}{\big\{\tau q_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)>0\big\}}

\displaystyle=ds_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)\mathbbold{1}{\big\{q_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)>0\big\}}.

Also note that:

\displaystyle\frac{2D(z,\rho;\pi)}{\mathcal{J}^{\pi,\rho,\tau}(z)^{1/2}}\mathbbold{1}{\big\{\mathcal{J}^{\pi,\rho,\tau}(z)>0\big\}}

\displaystyle=\frac{d\sqrt{\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{\pi_{j}q_{j}^{\rho}(x)}{\sqrt{q_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)}}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{j}^{\rho}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}])+(1-d)\sqrt{(1-\tau)\pi_{y}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}q_{y}^{\rho}(x)}\frac{\pi_{y}^{\ast}}{\pi_{y}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{y}^{\rho}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)}{\mathcal{J}^{\pi,\rho,\tau}(z)^{1/2}}\mathbbold{1}{\big\{\mathcal{J}^{\pi,\rho,\tau}(z)>0\big\}}

\displaystyle=d\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{q_{j}^{\rho}(x)}{q_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)}(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{j}^{\rho}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}])\mathbbold{1}{\big\{q_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)>0\big\}}+(1-d)\frac{\pi_{y}^{\ast}}{\pi_{y}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{y}^{\rho}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\mathbbold{1}{\big\{q_{y}^{\rho}(x)>0\big\}}.

Ergo:

\displaystyle S_{\pi,\rho}^{\tau}(z)

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}ds_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)\mathbbold{1}{\big\{q_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)>0\big\}}\\
d\sum\limits_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{q_{j}^{\rho}(x)}{q_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)}(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{j}^{\rho}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}])\mathbbold{1}{\big\{q_{\pi}^{\rho}(x)>0\big\}}+(1-d)\sum\limits_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{j}^{\rho}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\mathbbold{1}{\big\{q_{j}^{\rho}(x)>0\big\}}\end{pmatrix},

as claimed.

∎

Proof of Lemma (
6.18
)
.

First, we restate the definition of the semiparametric nuisance tangent set from
[
Newey, 1990
]
, but in the context of our fixed
\tau
IID regime. The semiparametric nuisance tangent set
\mathcal{T}
is defined as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}

\displaystyle:=\bigg\{t:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\times\{0,1\}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{m}\text{ }\bigg|\text{ }\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\big[||t(Z)||_{2}^{2}\big]<\infty,

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\text{ }\exists\{B^{(k)}\},\{\mathbf{M}^{\textup{sub},\tau,(k)}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{s.t.}\hskip 9.24994pt\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\big[||t(Z)-B^{(k)}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\tau,(k)}(Z)||_{2}^{2}\big]=0\bigg\}.

(51)

In the definition above,
\mathbf{M}^{\textup{sub},\tau,(k)}
denotes a smooth (in the sense of
[
Newey, 1990
]
) parametric submodel (of
\mathbf{M}^{\text{semi},\tau}
) containing densities
\mathcal{J}^{\pi,\rho,\tau,(k)}
,
\rho
is a finite dimensional vector that is used by
\mathbf{M}^{\textup{sub},\tau,(k)}
to parameterize a subset of
\mathcal{Q}
in a fashion that ensures
\exists\rho^{\ast}
such that
\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\rho^{\ast},\tau,(k)}=\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}
,
B^{(k)}
is a conformable matrix with
m
rows, and
S_{\rho}^{\tau,(k)}
is the score function for
\rho
evaluated at
(\pi^{\ast},\rho)
. Note that

\mathcal{J}^{\pi,\rho,\tau,(k)}(z)\equiv\big(\tau q_{\pi}^{\rho,(k)}(x)\big)^{d}\Bigg((1-\tau)\prod_{j=0}^{m}\big(\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)\big)^{\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}}\Bigg)^{1-d}\mathbbold{1}{\big\{x\in\mathcal{X},\text{ }y\in\mathcal{Y},\text{ }d\in\{0,1\}\big\}},

where
(q_{0}^{\rho,(k)},q_{1}^{\rho,(k)},\dots,q_{m}^{\rho,(k)})\in\mathcal{Q}
depends
\rho
. Also, for any given smooth parametric submodel
\mathbf{M}^{\textup{sub},\tau,(k)}
, one can use arguments similar to those employed in the proof of Lemma (
6.16
) to show that
S_{\rho}^{\tau,(k)}=\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\log\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\rho,\tau,(k)}(z)
a.s.
[\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\rho,\tau,(k)}]
. Now, observe that:

\displaystyle\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\log\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\rho,\tau,(k)}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\log\Bigg\{\big(\tau q_{\pi^{\ast}}^{\rho,(k)}(x)\big)^{d}\Big((1-\tau)\prod_{j=0}^{m}\big(\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)\big)^{\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}}\Big)^{(1-d)}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\Bigg\{d\log(\tau)+d\log q_{\pi^{\ast}}^{\rho,(k)}(x)+(1-d)\log(1-\tau)+(1-d)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\Big[\log(\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}})+\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)\Big]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\Bigg\{d\log q_{\pi^{\ast}}^{\rho,(k)}(x)+(1-d)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=d\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\log q_{\pi^{\ast}}^{\rho,(k)}(x)+(1-d)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)

\displaystyle=d\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)}{q_{\pi^{\ast}}^{\rho,(k)}(x)}+(1-d)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)

\displaystyle=d\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)}{q_{\pi^{\ast}}^{\rho,(k)}(x)}+(1-d)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)

\displaystyle=d\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)}{q_{\pi^{\ast}}^{\rho,(k)}(x)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)+(1-d)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x).

Ergo, the following holds a.s.
[\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\rho^{\ast},\tau}]
:

\displaystyle S_{\rho^{\ast}}(z)

\displaystyle=d\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}+(1-d)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}.

Now, we want to prove that
\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
. So, let any
t\in\mathcal{T}
be given. We will prove that
t\in\mathcal{G}
. Towards that end, let
\{B^{(k)}\},\{\mathbf{M}^{\textup{sub},\tau,(k)}\}
be the matrices and submodels used in the the definition of
t
, respectively. Observe that:

\displaystyle t(z)

\displaystyle\equiv t(x,y,d)

\displaystyle=dt(x,y,1)+(1-d)t(x,y,0)

\displaystyle=dt(x,y,1)+(1-d)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}t(x,j,0).

So, the difference
t-B^{(k)}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\tau,(k)}
can be written as follows:

\displaystyle t(z)-B^{(k)}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\tau,(k)}(z)

\displaystyle\equiv t(x,y,d)-B^{(k)}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\tau,(k)}(x,y,d)

\displaystyle=t(x,y,d)-d\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}+(1-d)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=d\Bigg[t(x,y,1)-\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+(1-d)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\Bigg[t(x,j,0)-B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}\Bigg].

Due to the indicators
d
and
\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}
, we therefore have that:

\displaystyle\Big|\Big|t(z)-B^{(k)}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\tau,(k)}(z)\Big|\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=d\bigg|\bigg|t(x,y,1)-\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}\bigg|\bigg|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+(1-d)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\bigg|\bigg|t(x,j,0)-B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(x)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}\bigg|\bigg|_{2}^{2},

and so, taking expectations w.r.t. the true distribution
\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Bigg[\Big|\Big|t(Z)-B^{(k)}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\tau,(k)}(Z)\Big|\Big|_{2}^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}\mid D=1}\Bigg[\bigg|\bigg|t(X,Y,1)-\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(X)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)}B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}\bigg|\bigg|_{2}^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad+(1-\tau)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}\mid D=0,Y=j}\Bigg[\bigg|\bigg|t(X,j,0)-B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}\bigg|\bigg|_{2}^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\Bigg[\bigg|\bigg|t(X,Y,1)-\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(X)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)}B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}\bigg|\bigg|_{2}^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad+(1-\tau)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}\Bigg[\bigg|\bigg|t(X,j,0)-B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}\bigg|\bigg|_{2}^{2}\Bigg].

(52)

Now, since
t\in\mathcal{T}
, we know that the sequences
\{B^{(k)}\}
and
\{\mathbf{M}^{\textup{sub},\tau,(k)}\}
satisfy
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Bigg[\Big|\Big|t(Z)-B^{(k)}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\tau,(k)}(Z)\Big|\Big|_{2}^{2}\Bigg]\to 0
as
k\to\infty
. Therefore, by display (
52
), it must be that
\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{j}\Bigg[\bigg|\bigg|t(X,j,0)-B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}\bigg|\bigg|_{2}^{2}\Bigg]=0
for each class
j
, and
\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\Bigg[\bigg|\bigg|t(X,Y,1)-\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(X)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)}B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}\bigg|\bigg|_{2}^{2}\Bigg]=0
as well. Now, observe that, for all
k
:

\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\Bigg[\bigg|\bigg|t(X,Y,1)-\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(X)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)}t(X,j,0)\bigg|\bigg|_{2}\Bigg]

\leq\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\Bigg[\bigg|\bigg|t(X,Y,1)-\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(X)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)}B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}\bigg|\bigg|_{2}\Bigg]

\hskip 17.70859pt\hskip 17.70859pt+\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\Bigg[\frac{p_{j}(X)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)}\bigg|\bigg|B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}-t(X,j,0)\bigg|\bigg|_{2}\Bigg]

=\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\Bigg[\bigg|\bigg|t(X,Y,1)-\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(X)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)}B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}\bigg|\bigg|_{2}\Bigg]+\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{j}\Bigg[\bigg|\bigg|B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}-t(X,j,0)\bigg|\bigg|_{2}\Bigg].

By our argument earlier, the two summands in the display above converge to zero as
k\to\infty
, so since the inequality holds for all
k
, it must be that
\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\Bigg[\bigg|\bigg|t(X,Y,1)-\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(X)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)}t(X,j,0)\bigg|\bigg|_{2}\Bigg]=0
. Thus, we may say that:

t(x,y,1)=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}t(x,j,0)\quad\text{ a.s. }[p_{\pi^{\ast}}],

meaning that:

t(z)=d\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}t(x,j,0)+(1-d)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}t(x,j,0)\quad\text{ a.s. }[\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}].

So, to show that
t\in\mathcal{G}
, it remains to show that
\mathbb{E}_{j}[t(X,j,0)]=0
and
\mathbb{E}_{j}\Big[\big|\big|t(X,j,0)\big|\big|_{2}^{2}\Big]<\infty
for each class
j
. The latter criteria is clearly true, because the fact that
t\in\mathcal{T}
means that
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\big[||t(Z)||_{2}^{2}\big]<\infty
, which since
\tau<1
and
\xi\leq\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}\leq 1-\xi
, can in turn only be true if
\mathbb{E}_{j}\Big[\big|\big|t(X,j,0)\big|\big|_{2}^{2}\Big]<\infty
for each
j
. To see that
\mathbb{E}_{j}[t(X,j,0)]=0
, observe that:

\displaystyle\Big|\Big|\mathbb{E}_{j}[t(X,j,0)]\Big|\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big|\Big|\mathbb{E}_{j}[t(X,j,0)]-0\Big|\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\Bigg|\mathbb{E}_{j}[t(X,j,0)]-\mathbb{E}_{j}\Big[B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}\Big]\Bigg|\Bigg|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{j}\Bigg[\bigg|\bigg|t(X,j,0)-B^{(k)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho,(k)}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}\bigg|\bigg|_{2}^{2}\Bigg].

Since the inequality above holds for all
k
and the last line goes to zero as
k\to\infty
, it must be that
\mathbb{E}_{j}[t(X,j,0)]=0
, as desired. Thus, we have shown that
t\in\mathcal{G}
. Hence,
\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
, as claimed.

∎

Proof of Lemma (
6.19
)
.

First, we will prove the eigenvalue bounds on
\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)
. Note that
\pi^{\ast},\pi^{\textup{\text{tr}}}\in\Delta
, which means that
\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)\in\Gamma
by virtue of Lemma (
6.1
) (this can be seen by setting
a=\frac{1}{\tau}
and
b=\frac{1}{1-\tau}
in the statement of Lemma (
6.1
)). Thus, by Assumption (
B1
), the matrix
\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))
is positive definite, and so its inverse in the definition of
\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)
is well defined. We start with the upper bound:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\textup{max}}\big(\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\lambda_{\textup{max}}\Big\{\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}-\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Big\}+\frac{1}{\tau}\lambda_{\textup{max}}\Big\{\mathcal{I}(\pi^{\ast};\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\lambda_{\textup{max}}\Big\{\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\Big\}+\frac{1}{\tau}\lambda_{\textup{max}}\Big\{\mathcal{I}(\pi^{\ast};\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\frac{1}{\lambda_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\big)}+\frac{1}{\tau}\frac{1}{\lambda_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\pi^{\ast};\textup{Cat})\big)}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Lambda}}+\frac{1}{2\tau}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{(1-\tau)\xi}\bigg]\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Lambda}}+\frac{1}{2\tau}.

The second line is because
\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)\in\Gamma
, so the components of
\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)
are all bounded away from
0
and
1
, and so by Lemma (
6.3
),
\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})^{-1}
is positive definite. The fourth line is by Assumption (
B1
) and Lemma (
6.3
), and the fifth line is because
\pi^{\ast},\pi^{\textup{\text{tr}}}\in\Delta
. As for the lower bound:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)\big)

\displaystyle\geq\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\lambda_{\textup{min}}\Big\{\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}-\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Big\}+\frac{1}{\tau}\lambda_{\textup{min}}\Big\{\mathcal{I}(\pi^{\ast};\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Big\}

\displaystyle\geq\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\lambda_{\textup{min}}\Big\{\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}-\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Big\}

\displaystyle\geq(\xi)^{2}\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\bigg]\lambda_{\textup{min}}\Big\{\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}-\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\xi)^{2}}{\tau(1-\tau)}\sigma_{\textup{min}}\Big\{\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}-\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\xi)^{2}}{\tau(1-\tau)}\sigma_{\textup{min}}\Big\{\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\big[\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})-\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\big]\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\text{Cat})^{-1}\Big\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(\xi)^{2}}{\tau(1-\tau)}\sigma_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\big)\sigma_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})-\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\big)\sigma_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\text{Cat})^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(\xi)^{2}\nu}{\tau(1-\tau)}\sigma_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\big)\sigma_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\text{Cat})^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\xi)^{2}\nu}{\tau(1-\tau)}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\textup{max}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\big)\sigma_{\textup{max}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\text{Cat})\big)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(\xi)^{2}\nu}{\tau(1-\tau)}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\textup{max}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\text{Cat})\big)^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(\xi)^{2}\nu}{\tau(1-\tau)}\bigg(\frac{L}{m+1}\bigg)^{2}.

The second line is by Lemma (
6.3
) and the fact that
\pi^{\ast}\in\Delta
. The fourth line is because each component of
\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)
is bounded away from
0
and
1
, and so by Lemma (
3.5
), the matrix
\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}-\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})^{-1}
is symmetric positive semidefinite. The seventh, ninth and tenth line all use the fact that
\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)\in\Gamma
; in addition, the seventh, ninth and tenth lines use Assumption (
B2
), Lemma (
3.5
) and Lemma (
6.3
), respectively.

Second, we need to prove that
ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\in\mathcal{G}
. To do that, we will show that the function

\bar{g}(z):=d\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\bar{f}_{j}(x)+(1-d)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\bar{f}_{j}(x)

where

\bar{f}_{j}(x):=\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)

satisfies
\bar{g}\in\mathcal{G}
, and furthermore, that
\bar{g}(z)=ds_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(z)
.

To see that
\bar{g}\in\mathcal{G}
, first observe that
\mathbb{E}_{j}[\bar{f}_{j}]=0
. One can also argue that
\mathbb{E}_{j}\big[||\bar{f}_{j}||_{2}^{2}\big]<\infty
, in the following manner. By the argument above, both
\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)
and
\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))
are symmetric positive definite. Therefore, it follows that:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\textup{max}}\big(\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\textup{max}}\Big\{\Big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)\Big)^{-1}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)\big)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\big)\lambda_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)\big)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Lambda}\frac{(\xi)^{2}\nu}{\tau(1-\tau)}\big(\frac{L}{m+1}\big)^{2}}

\displaystyle<\infty,

where the fourth line is by Assumption (
B1
) and the lower bound on
\lambda_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)\big)
that we just established. Now, since
||s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)||_{2}^{2}
is uniformly bounded over all
x\in\mathcal{X}
, the fact that
\lambda_{\textup{max}}\big(\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\big)<\infty
means that
||\bar{f}_{j}(x)||_{2}^{2}
is also uniformly bounded, too. Ergo, it must be that
\mathbb{E}_{j}\big[||\bar{f}_{j}||_{2}^{2}\big]<\infty
, as claimed. This shows that
\bar{g}\in\mathcal{G}
.

Having shown that
\bar{g}\in\mathcal{G}
, we now need to verify that
\bar{g}(z)
equals
ds_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(z)
. Towards that end, note that

\displaystyle\bar{g}(z)

\displaystyle\equiv d\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\bar{f}_{j}(x)+(1-d)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\bar{f}_{j}(x)

\displaystyle=d\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\bar{f}_{j}(x)+\frac{1-d}{1-\tau}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big).

Thus:

\bar{g}(z)=ds_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(z)

\iff\bar{g}(z)=ds_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\Bigg(\frac{d}{\tau}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)-\frac{1-d}{1-\tau}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\Bigg)

\iff d\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\bar{f}_{j}(x)=ds_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\Bigg(\frac{d}{\tau}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\Bigg).

(53)

So, to prove that
\bar{g}(z)=ds_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(z)
, we need to verify that display (
53
) holds. Towards that end, observe that:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\bar{f}_{j}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}].

Note that:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\bigg[\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\tau}\bigg]\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}-\sum_{j=0}^{m}\bigg[\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\tau}\bigg]\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\frac{1}{\tau}\bigg]\sum_{j=0}^{m}\gamma_{j}^{\ast}(\tau)\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}-\frac{1}{\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\frac{1}{\tau}\bigg]\frac{p_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}-\frac{1}{\tau}.

Thus:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\bar{f}_{j}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)\Bigg(\bigg[\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\frac{1}{\tau}\bigg]\frac{p_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}-\frac{1}{\tau}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\Bigg(\bigg[\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\frac{1}{\tau}\bigg]s_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-\frac{s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)}{\tau}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

(54)

Next, we will focus on the term
\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]
. Towards that end, note that:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\frac{1}{\tau}\bigg]\mathbb{E}_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\frac{1}{\tau}\bigg]\sum_{j=0}^{m}\gamma_{j}^{\ast}(\tau)\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\bigg[\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\tau}\bigg]\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]+\frac{1}{\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]+\frac{1}{\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}],

Thus:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

(55)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]-0

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]-\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]-\frac{1}{\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{p_{j}(x)-p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]-\frac{1}{\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}].

(56)

We will now show that

\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{p_{j}(x)-p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

=\Bigg(\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})^{-1}-\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathcal{I}(\pi^{\ast};\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Bigg)s_{\pi^{\ast}}(x).

Towards that end, it will be helpful to first derive the form of
\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathcal{I}(\beta;\textup{Cat})^{-1}
for arbitrary
\beta
. The
{(i,j)}^{\text{th}}
entry of this matrix product is equal to:

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathcal{I}(\beta;\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Big]_{ij}

\displaystyle=\Big\langle\textup{row }i\textup{ of }\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)),\textup{ col }j\textup{ of }\mathcal{I}(\beta;\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=\Big\langle\big(\mathbb{E}_{1}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]-\mathbb{E}_{0}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}],\dots,\mathbb{E}_{m}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]-\mathbb{E}_{0}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]\big),\big(-\beta_{1}\beta_{j},\dots,\beta_{j}(1-\beta_{j}),\dots,-\beta_{m}\beta_{j}\big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=-\beta_{j}\Big\langle\big(\mathbb{E}_{1}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]-\mathbb{E}_{0}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}],\dots,\mathbb{E}_{m}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]-\mathbb{E}_{0}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]\big),\big(\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{j}-1,\dots,\beta_{m}\big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=-\beta_{j}\Bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{m}\beta_{k}\big(\mathbb{E}_{k}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]-\mathbb{E}_{0}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]\big)-\big(\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]-\mathbb{E}_{0}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]\big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=-\beta_{j}\Bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{m}\beta_{k}\mathbb{E}_{k}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]-\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{m}\beta_{k}\bigg)\mathbb{E}_{0}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]+\mathbb{E}_{0}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]\Bigg)

\displaystyle=-\beta_{j}\Bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{m}\beta_{k}\mathbb{E}_{k}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]+\bigg(1-\sum_{k=1}^{m}\beta_{k}\bigg)\mathbb{E}_{0}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]\Bigg)

\displaystyle=-\beta_{j}\big(\mathbb{E}_{\beta}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau),i}]\big).

Hence, the
{j}^{\text{th}}
column of
\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathcal{I}(\beta;\textup{Cat})^{-1}
is given by:

\displaystyle\implies\Big[\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathcal{I}(\beta;\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Big]_{\cdot,j}=-\beta_{j}\big(\mathbb{E}_{\beta}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big).

Since this holds for general
\beta
, it follows that:

\displaystyle\Bigg[\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Bigg]_{\cdot,j}

\displaystyle=-\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\gamma_{j}^{\ast}(\tau)\big(\mathbb{E}_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\gamma_{j}^{\ast}(\tau)\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\tau}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}].

And also that:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau}\Big[\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathcal{I}(\pi^{\ast};\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Big]_{\cdot,j}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\tau}\big(\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]+\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\tau}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}].

Ergo:

\displaystyle\Bigg[\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})^{-1}-\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathcal{I}(\pi^{\ast};\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Bigg]_{\cdot,j}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]+\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}].

Now, note that:

\displaystyle p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-p_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=\bigg(1-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\bigg)p_{0}(x)+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}p_{j}(x)-p_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}p_{0}(x)+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}p_{j}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\big(p_{j}(x)-p_{0}(x)\big),

meaning that
\frac{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-p_{0}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}s_{\pi^{\ast},j}(x)
. Combining this with our matrix results, it follows that:

\Bigg[\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})^{-1}-\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathcal{I}(\pi^{\ast};\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Bigg]s_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)

=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\bigg(\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]+\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\bigg)s_{\pi^{\ast},j}(x)

=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]s_{\pi^{\ast},j}(x)+\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]}{\tau}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}s_{\pi^{\ast},j}(x)

=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]s_{\pi^{\ast},j}(x)+\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]}{\tau}\frac{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-p_{0}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}

=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\frac{p_{j}(x)-p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\frac{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-p_{0}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}+\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]}{\tau}\frac{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-p_{0}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}

=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\frac{p_{j}(x)-p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}+\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]+\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]}{\tau}\bigg)\frac{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-p_{0}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}.

Now, note that:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]+\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]}{\tau}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]+\frac{1}{\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]+\frac{1}{\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]-\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{0}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{0}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{0}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\bigg(\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\tau}\bigg)\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]-\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{0}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{0}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{0}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\mathbb{E}_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]-\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{0}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{0}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{0}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{0}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{0}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{0}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}].

Therefore, we have that:

\Bigg[\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})^{-1}-\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathcal{I}(\pi^{\ast};\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Bigg]s_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)

=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\frac{p_{j}(x)-p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)},

as claimed. Plugging in this identity into line (
56
), it follows that:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{p_{j}(x)-p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]-\frac{1}{\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\Bigg[\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})^{-1}-\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{I}(\pi^{\ast};\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Bigg]s_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-\frac{1}{\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}].

Plugging in the above into line (
54
), we have that:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\bar{f}_{j}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\Bigg(\bigg[\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\frac{1}{\tau}\bigg]s_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-\frac{s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)}{\tau}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\Bigg(\bigg[\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\frac{1}{\tau}\bigg]s_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-\frac{s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)}{\tau}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\Bigg[\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})^{-1}-\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{I}(\pi^{\ast};\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Bigg]s_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\frac{\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}}{\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\Bigg[\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg]\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}-\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})^{-1}\big)+\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{I}(\pi^{\ast};\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Bigg]s_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\frac{\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}}{\tau}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)s_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\frac{\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}}{\tau}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)

\displaystyle=s_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\frac{\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}}{\tau}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big).

This verifies the identity stated in display (
53
), meaning that, indeed, it is true that
\bar{g}(z)=ds_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(z)
. Consequentially, because we already proved that
\bar{g}\in\mathcal{G}
, it follows that
ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\in\mathcal{G}
, as claimed!

Finally, it remains for us to prove the following orthogonality condition:

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[g^{\prime}\big(S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\big(Ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)\big)\Big]=0\qquad\forall\ g\in\mathcal{G}.

By Lemma (
6.16
), this is equivalent to showing that

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[g^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\Big]=0\qquad\forall\ g\in\mathcal{G}.

Towards that end, consider any
g\in\mathcal{G}
, and let
f_{j}
denote the functions used in the definition of
g
. For
z=(x,y,d)
that are non-negligible under
\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}
, we have that:

\displaystyle g(z)^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(z)

\displaystyle=\Bigg(d\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}f_{j}(x)+(1-d)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}f_{j}(x)\Bigg)^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\times\Bigg(\frac{d}{\tau}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)-\frac{1-d}{1-\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{\tau}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}f_{j}(x)\Bigg)^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\Bigg(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt-\frac{1-d}{1-\tau}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}f_{j}(x)\Bigg)^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{\tau}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}f_{j}(x)\Bigg)^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\Bigg(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt-\frac{1-d}{1-\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\Bigg(f_{j}(x)\Bigg)^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\Bigg(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\Bigg)

Now, since
\mathbb{E}_{j}\big[f_{j}\big]=0
, we have that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Bigg[\frac{D}{\tau}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(X)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)}f_{j}(X)\Bigg)^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\Bigg[\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(X)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)}f_{j}(X)\Bigg)^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\Bigg[\frac{p_{j}(X)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)}f_{j}(X)\Bigg]\Bigg)^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{j}\big[f_{j}\big]\Bigg)^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=0.

Likewise, note that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Bigg[\frac{1-d}{1-\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}f_{j}(x)^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{j}\big[f_{j}\big]^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]

\displaystyle=0.

Ergo:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[g^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Bigg[\frac{D}{\tau}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(X)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)}f_{j}(X)\Bigg)^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Bigg[\frac{1-D}{1-\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{Y=j\}}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}f_{j}(X)^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\Bigg[\frac{p_{j}(X)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)}f_{j}(X)^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{j}\Bigg[f_{j}(X)^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{j}\Bigg[f_{j}(X)^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{j}\Bigg[f_{j}(X)^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=0,

meaning that the orthogonality condition holds.
∎

Proof of Corollary (
3.6
)
.

By Lemma (
6.18
), we know that
\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
, and so under Assumptions (
B2
) and (
B1
), it directly follows from Lemma (
6.19
) that

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[t^{\prime}\big(S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\big(Ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\text{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)\big)\Big]=0\qquad\forall\ t\in\mathcal{T}.

It remains for us to show that
ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\in\mathcal{T}
. With that goal in mind, consider the parametric submodel defined in Lemma (
6.17
), which is indeed a parametric submodel by virtue of Assumption (
B3
). Let
S_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}
denote the submodel’s score function for the nuisance parameter (i.e., components
m+1
to
2m
of
S_{\pi,\rho}^{\text{sub},\tau}
, as defined in Lemma (
6.17
)), evaluated at
(\pi^{\ast},0)
. Then for each
z
,
S_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}(z)
is given by

\displaystyle S_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}(z)

\displaystyle=d\sum\limits_{j=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{p_{j}(x)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)}(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}])\mathbbold{1}{\big\{p_{j}(x)>0\big\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+(1-d)\sum\limits_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\mathbbold{1}{\big\{p_{j}(x)>0\big\}}.

Now, by Lemma (
6.17
), the parametric submodel in question is smooth (in the sense of Definition A.1 in
[
Newey, 1990
]
), so by definition of
\mathcal{T}
(see the proof of Lemma (
6.18
)), if
C\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
satisfies
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\big[||CS_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}||_{2}^{2}\big]<\infty
, then
CS_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}\in\mathcal{T}
. Note that
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\big[||CS_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}||_{2}^{2}\big]\leq\lambda_{\textup{max}}\big(C\big)\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\big[||S_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}||_{2}^{2}\big]
and that
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\big[||S_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}||_{2}^{2}\big]<\infty
, so to show that
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\big[||CS_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}||_{2}^{2}\big]<\infty
, it suffices to show that
\lambda_{\textup{max}}\big(C\big)<\infty
.

Here, we will choose
C=\frac{1}{1-\tau}\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}
, and show that
CS_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}\in\mathcal{T}
by verifying that
\lambda_{\textup{max}}\big(C\big)<\infty
. Towards that end, observe that:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\textup{max}}\big(C\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\tau}\lambda_{\textup{max}}\Bigg\{\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\tau}\frac{1}{\lambda_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)\big)\lambda_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\big)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-\tau)\sqrt{\Lambda}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)\big)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-\tau)\sqrt{\Lambda}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{\textup{min}}\big(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{I}(\pi^{\ast};\textup{Cat})^{-1}\big)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau}{(1-\tau)\sqrt{\Lambda}}\lambda_{\textup{max}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\pi^{\ast};\textup{Cat})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tau}{(1-\tau)\sqrt{\Lambda}}\frac{m+1}{\xi}

\displaystyle<\infty.

where the second to last inequality follows from Lemma (
6.3
). Thus, by our argument earlier, we may conclude that
\frac{1}{1-\tau}\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}S_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}\in\mathcal{T}
.

Next, note that
\frac{1}{1-\tau}\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}S_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}(z)=\bar{g}(z)
where
\bar{g}(z)
was defined in the proof of Lemma (
6.19
). Also recall that, in the proof of Lemma (
6.19
), we proved that
\bar{g}(z)=ds_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(z)
. Consequentially, we have that
\frac{1}{1-\tau}\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}S_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}(z)=ds_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(z)
, and so
ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\in\mathcal{T}
, as claimed!

∎

Proof of Lemma (
3.7
)
.

To prove that
\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)
is the semiparametric efficiency bound and
\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}
is the efficient influence function for the fixed
\tau
-IID regime, we will verify the conditions of Theorem 3.2 in
[
Newey, 1990
]
. In our setting, these conditions are:

(A)

\mathcal{J}^{\pi,\mathbf{p},\tau}(z)
is smooth with score function
S_{\pi}(z)=ds_{\pi}(x)\mathbbold{1}{\{p_{\pi}(x)>0\}}
.

(B)

The residual of the projection of
S_{\pi^{\ast}}
on
\mathcal{T}
has a nonsingular covariance matrix.

As a quick aside, note that the Theorem 3.2 which appears in
[
Newey, 1990
]
additionally requires that
\mathcal{T}
be linear. However, as Newey points out (see
[
Newey, 1990
, p. 106]
), this is actually not a necessary condition– it is only used to ensure that the projection of
S_{\pi^{\ast}}
onto
\mathcal{T}
exists. Indeed, his proof of Theorem 3.2 does not depend on
\mathcal{T}
to be linear. For us, since we have already identified the projection of
S_{\pi^{\ast}}
onto
\mathcal{T}
in Corollary (
3.6
), we already know that the projection exists; as such, in this proof, we omit checking whether or not
\mathcal{T}
is linear.

Now, condition (A) is true by Lemma (
6.16
). Towards checking condition (B), we first find the covariance matrix of the residual of the projection of
S_{\pi^{\ast}}
on
\mathcal{T}
. Using the formula for this projection from Corollary (
3.6
), the covariance matrix in question is given by:

\displaystyle\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\big(Ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\Big]\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}.

where the first equality follows from Lemma (
6.16
). Now, note that:

\displaystyle\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Bigg[\frac{d}{\tau}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)-\frac{1-d}{1-\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Bigg[\frac{d}{\tau^{2}}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)^{\prime}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Bigg[\frac{1-d}{(1-\tau)^{2}}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\bigg)^{\prime}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Bigg[\frac{d}{\tau^{2}}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)^{\prime}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Bigg[\frac{1-d}{(1-\tau)^{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{(\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}})^{2}}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)^{\prime}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\Bigg[\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)^{\prime}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}\Bigg[\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)^{\prime}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau}\textup{\text{Var}}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\textup{\text{Var}}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}].

Now, by a derivation nearly identical to the work done in the proof of Corollaries (
2.1
) and (
6.7.2
), the last line in the display above equals:

\displaystyle\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\Bigg]\Big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))-\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\Big)+\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\mathcal{I}(\pi^{\ast};\textup{Cat})^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)).

This implies that:

\displaystyle\implies\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\Big]\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{1}{1-\tau}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\Bigg]\Big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}-\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau);\textup{Cat})^{-1}\Big)+\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{I}(\pi^{\ast};\textup{Cat})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau),

(57)

as was claimed in the statement of the present Lemma. Hence, it follows that

\displaystyle\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\big(Ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\Big]\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1},

(58)

which since
\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)
can be shown to be nonsingular, implies the nonsingularity of
\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}
and hence of
\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\big(Ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)\Big]
.

Thus, both conditions (A) and (B) are met. So, by Theorem 3.2 in
[
Newey, 1990
]
, it follows that the efficient influence function is

\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\big(Ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)\Big]^{-1}\Big(S_{\pi^{\ast}}(z)-\big(ds_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(z)\big)\Big)
. By line (
58
) and Lemma (
6.16
), this can be rewritten as:

\displaystyle\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\big(Ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)\Big]^{-1}\Big(S_{\pi^{\ast}}(z)-\big(ds_{\pi^{\ast}}(x)-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(z)\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(z)

\displaystyle=\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(z),

so
\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(z)
is indeed the efficient influence function. Theorem 3.2 in
[
Newey, 1990
]
also tells us that the efficiency bound is given by
\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\big(Ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)\Big]^{-1}
, which by line (
58
), is equal to
\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)
.

Thus, we have proven that
\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}
is the efficient influence function and that
\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)
is the efficiency bound!

∎

Proof of Lemma (
6.20
)
.

First, we compute the form of
\textup{CRLB}\big(\mathbf{M}^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}\big)
for an arbitrary smooth parametric submodel
\mathbf{M}^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}
. Let
\Theta^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}}}
denote the parameter space for the submodel, and let
\theta=(\pi,\rho)\in\Theta^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}}}
denote an arbitrary member of that space. Let
k
denote the number of components in the vector
\theta
. The true parameter is
\theta^{\ast}=(\pi^{\ast},\rho^{\ast})
. We are interested in learning only
\pi^{\ast}
, not
\rho^{\ast}
; this is equivalent to learning
[I_{m\times m},\ 0_{m\times k-m}]\theta^{\ast}
. The CRLB for learning this function of
\pi^{\ast}
is:

\displaystyle\textup{CRLB}\big(\mathbf{M}^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}\big)

\displaystyle=[I_{m\times m},\ 0_{m\times k-m}]\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\big[S_{\pi,\rho}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}\big]^{-1}[I_{m\times m},\ 0_{m\times k-m}]^{\prime},

where
S_{\pi,\rho}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}=\big[S_{\pi^{\ast}},S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}\big]
is the submodel’s
\mathbb{R}^{k}
-valued score function for both the parameter of interest and the nuisance parameter, evaluated at the truth. Now, define

K^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}:=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[S_{\pi^{\ast}}\big(S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}\big)^{\prime}\Big]\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}\Big]^{-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times k}.

Then, by the partitioned inverse formula, it follows that:

\displaystyle\textup{CRLB}\big(\mathbf{M}^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}\big)

\displaystyle=\bigg\{\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}[S_{\pi^{\ast}}]-K^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}S_{\pi^{\ast}}^{\prime}\Big]\bigg\}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}[S_{\pi^{\ast}}S_{\pi^{\ast}}^{\prime}]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[K^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}S_{\pi^{\ast}}^{\prime}\Big]-0\bigg\}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[\big(S_{\pi^{\ast}}-K^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}\big)S_{\pi^{\ast}}^{\prime}\Big]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[\big(S_{\pi^{\ast}}-K^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}\big)\big(K^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}\big)^{\prime}\Big]\bigg\}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[S_{\pi^{\ast}}-K^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}\Big]^{-1},

where the second to last line is because
S_{\pi^{\ast}}-K^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}
is the residual from the mean-square projection of
S_{\pi^{\ast}}
onto the linear space spanned by
S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}
, meaning that
S_{\pi^{\ast}}-K^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}
is orthogonal to
K^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}
.

Next, observe that:

\displaystyle\textup{CRLB}\big(\mathbf{M}^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}\big)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[S_{\pi^{\ast}}-K^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}\Big]

\displaystyle=\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\bigg[\Big(\underbrace{S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\big(Ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)}_{=:W_{1}}\Big)+\Big(\underbrace{\big(Ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)-K^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}}_{=:W_{2}}\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}[W_{1}+W_{2}].

From the definition of
\mathcal{T}
in Lemma (
6.18
), we know that
S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}\in\mathcal{T}
; further, since
\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
by Lemma (
6.18
), we know that
S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}\in\mathcal{G}
. Now, it is easily seen that
\mathcal{G}
is linear, so since
ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\in\mathcal{G}
by Lemma (
6.19
), it follows that
W_{2}=\big(ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)-K^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\widetilde{\text{sub}},\tau}\in\mathcal{G}
as well. It is also easy to see that
\mathcal{G}
is a
m
-self replicating linear space (in the sense of
[
Tsiatis, 2006
, p. 44]
). Also,

W_{1}
is orthogonal to
\mathcal{G}
by Lemma (
6.19
). Thus, all the conditions of the Multivariate Pythagorean Theorem (Theorem 3.3 in
Tsiatis, 2006
) are met, and so it holds that

\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}[W_{1}+W_{2}]=\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}[W_{1}]+\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}[W_{2}]

Consequentially:

\displaystyle\textup{CRLB}\big(\mathbf{M}^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}\big)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}[W_{1}+W_{2}]

\displaystyle\succeq\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}[W_{1}]

\displaystyle=\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\big(Ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)\Big].

(59)

Next, we will argue that, in fact,
\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}[S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\big(Ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)]=\textup{CRLB}\big(\mathbf{M}^{\textup{sub},\tau}\big)^{-1}
. By our work earlier in this proof, this is equivalent to showing that

\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}[S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\big(Ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)]=\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[S_{\pi^{\ast}}-K^{\textup{sub},\tau}S_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}\Big],

where
S_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}
is the score function for the nuisance parameter in the submodel
\mathbf{M}^{\textup{sub},\tau}
and is equal to the last
m
components of the random vector defined in Lemma (
6.17
) evaluated at the truth
(\pi,\rho)=(\pi^{\ast},0)
, and
K^{\textup{sub},\tau}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
is such that
K^{\textup{sub},\tau}S_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}
is the mean-square projection of
S_{\pi^{\ast}}
onto
\big\{KS_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}\ :\ K\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}\big\}
. Now, by the argument employed at the very end of the proof of Corollary (
3.6
), we know that
\frac{1}{1-\tau}\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}S_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}=ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}
, and so to show that the display above is true, it suffices to show that

S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\frac{1}{1-\tau}\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}S_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}=S_{\pi^{\ast}}-K^{\textup{sub},\tau}S_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau},

i.e., it suffices to show that:

\frac{1}{1-\tau}\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}=K^{\textup{sub},\tau}.

(60)

To verify the equality in the display above, notice that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\bigg[\Big|\Big|S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\frac{1}{1-\tau}\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}S_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}\Big|\Big|_{2}^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\bigg[\Big|\Big|S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\big(Ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)\Big|\Big|_{2}^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\bigg[\Big|\Big|S_{\pi^{\ast}}-g\Big|\Big|_{2}^{2}\bigg]\quad\forall g\in\mathcal{G},

where the last line follows from Lemma (
6.19
). However, note that for any
K\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
, we have that
KS_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}\in\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
, and so the display above implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\bigg[\Big|\Big|S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\frac{1}{1-\tau}\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}S_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}\Big|\Big|_{2}^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\bigg[\Big|\Big|S_{\pi^{\ast}}-KS_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}\Big|\Big|_{2}^{2}\bigg]\quad\forall K\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m},

which means that the equality in display (
60
) is indeed correct, because
\frac{1}{1-\tau}\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}S_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}
is the matrix
K
for which the residual
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\bigg[\Big|\Big|S_{\pi^{\ast}}-KS_{0}^{\text{sub},\tau}\Big|\Big|_{2}^{2}\bigg]
is the smallest. As was reasoned earlier, this implies that
\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}[S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\big(Ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)]=\textup{CRLB}\big(\mathbf{M}^{\textup{sub},\tau}\big)^{-1}
. So, by line (
59
), it follows that

\displaystyle\textup{CRLB}\big(\mathbf{M}^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}\big)^{-1}

\displaystyle\succeq\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\big(Ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\textup{CRLB}\big(\mathbf{M}^{\textup{sub},\tau}\big)^{-1},

implying that
\textup{CRLB}\big(\mathbf{M}^{\widetilde{\textup{sub}},\tau}\big)\preceq\textup{CRLB}\big(\mathbf{M}^{\textup{sub},\tau}\big)
, as claimed by the present Lemma! Lastly, notice that, by line (
58
), we have that
\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\Big[S_{\pi^{\ast}}-\big(Ds_{\pi^{\ast}}-\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}\big)\Big]=\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}
, so
\textup{CRLB}\big(\mathbf{M}^{\textup{sub},\tau}\big)^{-1}=\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)^{-1}
, i.e.,
\textup{CRLB}\big(\mathbf{M}^{\textup{sub},\tau}\big)=\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)
, as was also claimed.

∎

6.4
\tau_{n}
-IID Regime

6.4.1
Assumptions

We assume that the sequence
\tau_{n}
satisfies Assumptions (
8
) and (
9
). We also assume Assumption (
6
), which concerns the complexity of the space
\mathcal{H}
. Finally, we assume access to an estimator
\widehat{\gamma}
that satisfies conditions in (
7
). All of the foregoing assumptions can be found in the Theoretical Results section of the paper.

6.4.2
Definition of
\mathcal{E}

The family of quantifiers
\mathcal{E}
is defined in the Theoretical Results section of the paper (see Definitions (
C1
), (
C2a
) and (
C2b
)).

6.4.3
Lemmas

Lemma 6.21

(
Asymptotics of a Sample Proportion
)
.

Let
\varphi_{n}\in(0,1)
be a sequence with the property that
n\varphi_{n}\to\infty
. For each
n
, let
\zeta_{1,n},\dots,\zeta_{n,n}\overset{\text{iid}}{\sim}\textup{Bern}(\varphi_{n})
and define

\widehat{\varphi}_{n}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\zeta_{i,n}.

Then, the following asymptotic statements are true:

\frac{\widehat{\varphi}_{n}-\varphi_{n}}{\varphi_{n}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n\varphi_{n}}}\bigg)

\frac{\widehat{\varphi}_{n}-\varphi_{n}}{\widehat{\varphi}_{n}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n\varphi_{n}}}\bigg)

\frac{1}{n\widehat{\varphi}_{n}}=\frac{1}{n\varphi_{n}}+O_{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\frac{1}{(n\varphi_{n})^{3/2}}\bigg)

Lemma 6.22

(
Rate of
|\gamma_{j}^{\ast}-\gamma_{j}^{\ast}(\tau_{n})|
)
.

Define the random vector
\gamma^{\ast}:=(\gamma_{1}^{\ast},\dots,\gamma_{m}^{\ast})
where

\gamma_{j}^{\ast}:=\frac{\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{N^{\textup{\text{te}}}}+\frac{1}{N^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\frac{(\pi_{j}^{\ast})^{2}}{\widehat{\pi}_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}}{\frac{1}{N^{\textup{\text{te}}}}+\frac{1}{N^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{(\pi_{k}^{\ast})^{2}}{\widehat{\pi}_{k}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}}\qquad\forall\ j\in[m],

(61)

Then, under Assumptions (
8
) and (
9
), we have for each
j\in[m]
that:

|\gamma_{j}^{\ast}-\gamma_{j}^{\ast}(\tau_{n})|=O_{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})}}\bigg).

Lemma 6.23

(
Error of
s_{\gamma^{\ast}}
vs.
s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}
in
\tau_{n}
-IID Regime
)
.

Under Assumptions (
8
) and (
9
), we have that

\displaystyle\Bigg|\Bigg|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{D_{i}}{\sqrt{\tau_{n}}}\Big[\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}}(X_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}}]\big)-\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}(X_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}]\big)\Big]\bigg/\sum_{k=0}^{m}\big|\gamma_{k}^{\ast}-\gamma_{k}^{\ast}(\tau_{n})\big|\Bigg|\Bigg|_{2}

is
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and

\displaystyle\Bigg|\Bigg|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(1-D_{i})\mathbbold{1}{\{Y_{i}=j\}}}{\sqrt{(1-\tau_{n})\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}}\Big[\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}(X_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}]\big)-\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}}(X_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}}]\big)\Big]\bigg/\sum_{k=0}^{m}\big|\gamma_{k}^{\ast}-\gamma_{k}^{\ast}(\tau_{n})\big|\Bigg|\Bigg|_{2}

is
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
for each
j\in[m]
.

Lemma 6.24

(
Lower Bound on
\lambda_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast})\big)
)
.

Under Assumption (
B1
), we have that

\lambda_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast})\big)>\Lambda\frac{L^{2}}{m^{2}}

with probability
1
.

Lemma 6.25

(
\widehat{\pi}_{n}
is
\tau
-RAL w.r.t
\textbf{M}^{\text{semi},\tau}
in Fixed
\tau
-IID Regime
)
.

Under Assumptions (
B1
), (
B2
), (
B3
), (
4
), (
5
), (
6
) and (
7
), the estimator
\widehat{\pi}_{n}
is regular in the Fixed
\tau
-IID Regime, as well as asymptotically linear with influence function
\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}
and second order error
\delta_{\tau}
.

6.4.4
Proofs

Proof of Lemma (
6.21
)
.

First, we will prove that
\frac{\widehat{\varphi}_{n}-\varphi_{n}}{\varphi_{n}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n\varphi_{n}}}\Big)
. Observe that:
