\mathbf{p}\in\mathcal{Q}
, it therefore follows from Assumption (
B1
) that
\sigma_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast})\big)>\sqrt{\Lambda}
for all sufficiently large
n
.

•

Assumption (
A7
) in the Fixed Sequence Regime is satisfied by Assumption (
7
) in the
\tau_{n}
-IID Regime, whenever
n
is sufficiently large and we condition on the event that
N^{\textup{\text{te}}}=\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{te}}}
and
N_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}=\bar{n}_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}\ \forall j\in\mathcal{Y}
. To see why, note that under Assumption (
7
),
\widehat{\gamma}\in\Gamma
for each
n
. Furthermore, conditional on the aforementioned event,
\gamma^{\ast}
is equal to
\bar{\gamma}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\textup{\text{II}}}
is equal to
\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\textup{\text{II}}}
, where
\bar{\gamma}
and
\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\textup{\text{II}}}
are defined in the Assumptions section of the
\tau_{n}
-IID Regime. Thus, indeed, it holds that
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}^{\textup{\text{II}}}}\Big[||\widehat{\gamma}-\gamma^{\ast}||_{2}^{2}\Big]\to 0
and, for each
a>0
,
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}^{\textup{\text{II}}}}\big[||\widehat{\gamma}-\gamma^{\ast}||_{2}\geq a\big]=o\Big(\sqrt{\frac{1}{\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{te}}}}+\frac{1}{\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}}\Big)
. Thus, Assumption (
A7
) holds.

Thus, we have shown that the data generation mechanism / assumptions of the
\tau_{n}
-IID Regime are a special case of the data generation mechanism / assumptions of the Fixed Sequence Regime, whenever
n
is sufficiently large and we condition on the event that
N^{\textup{\text{te}}}=\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{te}}}
and
N_{y}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}=\bar{n}_{y}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}
for each
y\in[m]
. Thus, we may invoke Corollary (
6.15.1
) from the Fixed Sequence Regime to make the following asymptotic statement about the distribution of
||\epsilon||_{2}
in the
\tau_{n}
-IID Regime, conditional on the event that
N^{\textup{\text{te}}}=\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{te}}}
and
N_{y}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}=\bar{n}_{y}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}
for each
y\in[m]
and
n
:

\displaystyle||\epsilon||_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Bigg(\sqrt{\bigg(\frac{1}{\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{te}}}}+\frac{1}{\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg)\bigg\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}}+\max_{y}\lambda_{y}\Omega(s_{\bar{\gamma},y})^{2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\min_{y}\lambda_{y}}}e^{-C\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\max_{y}\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\textup{\text{tr,II}}}}\Big[\bar{\mathcal{U}}_{y}(\lambda_{y})\Big]+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\textup{\text{II}}}}\Big[||\widehat{\gamma}-\bar{\gamma}||_{2}^{2}\Big]}\bigg\}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.70859pt+O_{\mathbb{P}}\Bigg(e^{-C(\min_{y}\lambda_{y})^{2}\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\mathbb{P}_{\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\textup{\text{II}}}}\Big[\big|\big|\widehat{\gamma}-\bar{\gamma}\big|\big|_{2}\geq C\Big]\Bigg),

where
C>0
is some fixed, global constant. Now, notice that, due to the constraints on
\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{te}}},\bar{n}_{0}^{\textup{\text{tr}}},\dots,\bar{n}_{m}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}
, it holds that:

\displaystyle\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})n\bigg(\frac{1}{\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{te}}}}+\frac{1}{\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=(1-\tau_{n})\frac{\tau_{n}}{\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{te}}}/n}+\tau_{n}\frac{1-\tau_{n}}{\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}/n}\to 1,

so:

\displaystyle\sqrt{\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})n}||\epsilon||_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Bigg(\sqrt{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}}+\max_{y}\lambda_{y}\Omega(s_{\bar{\gamma},y})^{2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\min_{y}\lambda_{y}}}e^{-C\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\max_{y}\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\textup{\text{tr,II}}}}\Big[\bar{\mathcal{U}}_{y}(\lambda_{y})\Big]+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\textup{\text{II}}}}\Big[||\widehat{\gamma}-\bar{\gamma}||_{2}^{2}\Big]}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+O_{\mathbb{P}}\Bigg(\sqrt{\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})n}e^{-C(\min_{y}\lambda_{y})^{2}\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\sqrt{\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})n}\mathbb{P}_{\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\textup{\text{II}}}}\Big[\big|\big|\widehat{\gamma}-\bar{\gamma}\big|\big|_{2}\geq C\Big]\Bigg),

which, by our arguments from earlier, is
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Now, set
b_{n}
equal to:

\displaystyle b_{n}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}}+\max_{y}\lambda_{y}\Omega(s_{\bar{\gamma},y})^{2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\min_{y}\lambda_{y}}}e^{-C\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\max_{y}\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\textup{\text{tr,II}}}}\Big[\bar{\mathcal{U}}_{y}(\lambda_{y})\Big]+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\textup{\text{II}}}}\Big[||\widehat{\gamma}-\bar{\gamma}||_{2}^{2}\Big]}}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+\sqrt{\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})n}e^{-C(\min_{y}\lambda_{y})^{2}\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\sqrt{\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})n}\mathbb{P}_{\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\textup{\text{II}}}}\Big[\big|\big|\widehat{\gamma}-\bar{\gamma}\big|\big|_{2}\geq C\Big].

Then, by definition of
O_{\mathbb{P}}(\cdot)
, it follows that

\mathbb{P}_{Z_{1:n}}\Big[\sqrt{\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})n}||\epsilon||_{2}/b_{n}\geq b\ \Big\lvert\ N^{\textup{\text{te}}}=\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{te}}},\ N_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}=\bar{n}_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}\ \forall j\in\mathcal{Y}\Big]

can be made arbitrarily small by choosing
b
and
n
to be sufficiently large. Next, we will argue that the probabilities

•

\mathbb{P}_{Z_{1:n}}\Big[|N^{\textup{\text{te}}}-\tau_{n}n|>c^{\textup{\text{te}}}\sqrt{\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})n}\Big]

•

\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbb{P}_{Z_{1:n}}\Big[|N_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}-(1-\tau_{n})\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}n|>c_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}\sqrt{(1-\tau_{n})\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}[1-(1-\tau_{n})\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}]n}\Big]

can be made arbitrarily small, whenever
n
and
c^{\textup{\text{te}}},c_{0}^{\textup{\text{tr}}},\dots,c_{m}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}
are taken to be sufficiently large. This is simple to prove. For each
n
,
N^{\textup{\text{te}}}\sim\text{Bin}(n,\ \tau_{n})
and
N_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}\sim\text{Bin}(n,\ (1-\tau_{n})\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}})
. Thus, it follows that
(N^{\textup{\text{te}}}-\tau_{n}n)/\sqrt{n\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723ptd\hskip 4.09723pt}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
and
(N_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}-(1-\tau_{n})\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}n)/\sqrt{(1-\tau_{n})\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}[1-(1-\tau_{n})\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}]n}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723ptd\hskip 4.09723pt}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. So, by definition of convergence in distribution, it follows that

•

\mathbb{P}_{Z_{1:n}}\Big[|N^{\textup{\text{te}}}-\tau_{n}n|>c^{\textup{\text{te}}}\sqrt{\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})n}\Big]\to 2\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{N}(0,1)\geq c^{\textup{\text{te}}}]

•

\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbb{P}_{Z_{1:n}}\Big[|N_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}-(1-\tau_{n})\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}n|>c_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}\sqrt{(1-\tau_{n})\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}[1-(1-\tau_{n})\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}]n}\Big]\to 2\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{N}(0,1)\geq c_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}]
.

Clearly, the limits can be made arbitrarily small by choosing
c^{\textup{\text{te}}},c_{0}^{\textup{\text{tr}}},\dots,c_{m}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}
to be sufficiently large. Since the display above implies that the probabilities on the LHS can be made arbitrarily close to their limits whenever
n
is sufficiently large, it follows that those probabilities can be made arbitrarily small by choosing
n
and
c^{\textup{\text{te}}},c_{0}^{\textup{\text{tr}}},\dots,c_{m}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}
to be sufficiently large, as claimed.

Now, set
b=c^{\textup{\text{te}}}=c_{0}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}=\dots=c_{m}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}
. Then, bringing all of our work together, it follows from line (
66
) that
\mathbb{P}_{Z_{1:n}}\big[||\delta_{2,\tau_{n}}(Z_{1:n})||_{2}/b_{n}\geq b\big]
can be made arbitrarily small by choosing
b
and
n
to be sufficiently large. This implies that:

\displaystyle||\delta_{2,\tau_{n}}(Z_{1:n})||_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(b_{n})

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Bigg(\sqrt{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}}+\max_{y}\lambda_{y}\Omega(s_{\bar{\gamma},y})^{2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\min_{y}\lambda_{y}}}e^{-C\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\max_{y}\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\textup{\text{tr,II}}}}\Big[\bar{\mathcal{U}}_{y}(\lambda_{y})\Big]+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\textup{\text{II}}}}\Big[||\widehat{\gamma}-\bar{\gamma}||_{2}^{2}\Big]}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+O_{\mathbb{P}}\Bigg(\sqrt{\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})n}e^{-C(\min_{y}\lambda_{y})^{2}\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\sqrt{\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})n}\mathbb{P}_{\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\textup{\text{II}}}}\Big[\big|\big|\widehat{\gamma}-\bar{\gamma}\big|\big|_{2}\geq C\Big]\Bigg).

Since we already showed that
\big|\big|\delta_{1,\tau_{n}}(Z_{1:n})\big|\big|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})}}\Big)
, we may conclude from line (
63
) that:

\displaystyle\sqrt{\tau_{n}(1-\tau)n}(\widehat{\pi}_{n}-\pi^{\ast})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})}{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{\tau_{n}}^{\textup{eff}}(Z_{i})+\delta_{\tau_{n}}(Z_{1:n})

where

\displaystyle\big|\big|\delta_{\tau_{n}}(Z_{1:n})\big|\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big|\big|\delta_{1,\tau_{n}}(Z_{1:n})\big|\big|_{2}+\big|\big|\delta_{2,\tau_{n}}(Z_{1:n})\big|\big|_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})}}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}}+\max_{y}\lambda_{y}\Omega(s_{\bar{\gamma},y})^{2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\min_{y}\lambda_{y}}}e^{-C\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\max_{y}\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\textup{\text{tr,II}}}}\Big[\bar{\mathcal{U}}_{y}(\lambda_{y})\Big]+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\textup{\text{II}}}}\Big[||\widehat{\gamma}-\bar{\gamma}||_{2}^{2}\Big]}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.70859pt\hskip 17.70859pt+O_{\mathbb{P}}\Bigg(\sqrt{\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})n}e^{-C(\min_{y}\lambda_{y})^{2}\bar{n}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}+\sqrt{\tau_{n}(1-\tau_{n})n}\mathbb{P}_{\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\textup{\text{II}}}}\Big[\big|\big|\widehat{\gamma}-\bar{\gamma}\big|\big|_{2}\geq C\Big]\Bigg),

as desired! Finally, we turn to verifying the asymptotic properties of
\psi_{\tau_{n}}^{\textup{eff}}
that were claimed by the present lemma. Observe that, for each
x\in\mathcal{X}
:

\displaystyle\big|\big|\tau_{n}\psi_{\tau_{n}}^{\textup{eff}}(x,-1,1)\big|\big|_{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg|\bigg|\tau_{n}\frac{1}{\tau_{n}}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n}))^{-1}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}]\big)\bigg|\bigg|_{2}

\displaystyle=\big|\big|\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n}))^{-1}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}]\big)\big|\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{\textup{max}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n}))^{-1}\big)\big|\big|s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}]\big|\big|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n}))\big)}\big|\big|s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}]\big|\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Lambda}}\big|\big|s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}]\big|\big|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Lambda}}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n}),j}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n}),j}]\Big)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Lambda}}\frac{2\sqrt{m}}{L},

where the third to last line uses Assumption (
5
) and the fact that
\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})\in\Gamma
by virtue of Lemma (
6.1
), and the last line uses Assumption (
5
). This implies that

\displaystyle\frac{(1-{\tau}_{n})^{2}}{{\tau}_{n}n}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\Big[\big|\big|\tau_{n}\psi_{\tau_{n}}^{\textup{eff}}(X,-1,1)\big|\big|_{2}^{4}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1-{\tau}_{n})^{2}}{{\tau}_{n}n}\frac{16m^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{{\tau}_{n}n}\frac{16m^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}L^{4}}\to 0,

where the limit is because of Assumption (
9
). Similarly, observe that, for each
x\in\mathcal{X}
and
y\in\mathcal{Y}
:

\displaystyle\big|\big|(1-{\tau}_{n})\psi^{\textup{eff}}_{{\tau}_{n}}(x,y,0)\big|\big|_{2}

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\Bigg|(1-{\tau}_{n})\frac{1}{1-\tau_{n}}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n}))^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}]\big)\Bigg|\Bigg|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sigma_{\textup{min}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n}))\big)}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{y=j\}}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\big|\big|s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau_{n})}]\big|\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Lambda}}\frac{1-\xi}{\xi}\frac{2m^{3/2}}{L}.

This implies that:

\displaystyle\frac{{\tau}_{n}^{2}}{(1-{\tau}_{n})n}\sum_{y=0}^{m}\pi_{y}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}\mathbb{E}_{y}\Big[||(1-{\tau}_{n})\psi^{\textup{eff}}_{{\tau}_{n}}(X,y,0)||_{2}^{4}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-{\tau}_{n})n}\frac{1}{\Lambda^{2}}\frac{1-\xi}{\xi}\frac{16m^{6}}{L^{4}}\to 0,

where the limit is because of Assumption (
9
). Overall, it follows that:

\frac{(1-{\tau}_{n})^{2}}{{\tau}_{n}n}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\Big[||{\tau}_{n}\psi^{\textup{eff}}_{{\tau}_{n}}(X,-1,1)||_{2}^{4}\Big]+\frac{{\tau}_{n}^{2}}{(1-{\tau}_{n})n}\sum_{y=0}^{m}\pi_{y}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}\mathbb{E}_{y}\Big[||(1-{\tau}_{n})\psi^{\textup{eff}}_{{\tau}_{n}}(X,y,0)||_{2}^{4}\Big]\to 0,

as desired!

∎

Proof of Lemma (
6.25
)
.

To prove the desideratum, we will verify the conditions of Theorem 2.2 in
[
Newey, 1990
]
. Since in our semiparametric model each
\theta\equiv(\pi,\mathbf{q})\in\Theta^{\text{semi}}
can be partitioned into a parametric component of interest,
\pi
, and a nonparametric nuisance component,
\mathbf{q}
, it suffices to show the following statements hold:

•

\widehat{\pi}_{n}
is asymptotically linear with influence function
\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}
.

•

For each regular parametric submodel
\mathbf{M}^{\text{sub},\tau}
with true parameter
\theta^{\ast}
,
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta,\tau}}\big[||\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}||_{2}^{2}\big]
exists and is continuous on a neighborhood of
\theta^{\ast}
.

•

For each regular parametric submodel
\mathbf{M}^{\text{sub},\tau}
with true parameter
\theta^{\ast}
,
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta^{\ast},\tau}}\big[\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}S_{\pi^{\ast}}^{\prime}\big]=I_{m\times m}
, and
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta^{\ast},\tau}}\big[\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\prime}\big]
is a matrix of all zeros.

First, we will prove that the asymptotic linearity condition holds. Recall that the Fixed
\tau
-IID Regime is a special case of the
\tau_{n}
-IID Regime, wherein
\tau_{n}=\tau
for each
n
. Thus, under Assumptions (
4
), (
5
), (
B2
), (
B1
), (
6
) and (
7
), we can apply Lemma (
3.8
) to the Fixed
\tau
-IID Regime. From this, it follows that:

\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\pi}_{n}-\pi^{\ast})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(Z_{i})+\delta_{\tau}(Z_{1:n})

where it is easily seen that
||\delta_{\tau}(Z_{1:n})||_{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. We can also verify that
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}[\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}]=0
, as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}[\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}}\bigg[\frac{D}{\tau}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)-\frac{1-D}{1-\tau}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{Y=j\}}\frac{\pi_{j}^{\ast}}{\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\bigg[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\bigg]-\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{j}\bigg[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\bigg]

\displaystyle=0.

In addition, under Assumptions (
B2
), (
B1
) and (
B3
), we have by Lemma (
3.7
) that
\textup{\text{Var}}_{\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau_{n}}}[\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}]=\mathbf{V}^{\textup{eff}}(\tau)
, the eigenvalues of which are bounded away from
0
and
\infty
due to Lemma (
6.19
). Thus, we have verified all the conditions necessary for showing that
\widehat{\pi}_{n}
is asymptotically linear with influence function
\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}
.

Second, we will prove that, if
\mathbf{M}^{\text{sub},\tau}
is a regular parametric submodel with nuisance parameter
\rho
, and
\theta^{\ast}=(\pi^{\ast},\rho^{\ast})
is such that
\mathcal{J}^{\theta^{\ast},\tau}=\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}
, then
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta,\tau}}\big[||\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}||_{2}^{2}\big]
exists and is continuous on a neighborhood of
\theta^{\ast}
. For any
\theta
, the existence of
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta,\tau}}\big[||\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}||_{2}^{2}\big]
can be established by arguing that
||\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}||_{2}
is a bounded function. To see this, note that
\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)\in\Gamma
by Lemma (
6.1
), and so by Assumption (
B1
) we have that
\sigma_{\textup{max}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\big)<1/\sqrt{\Lambda}<\infty
. Further, the fact that
\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)\in\Gamma
also implies that
||s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}||_{2}
is a bounded function. Thus, by inspecting the definition of
\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}
, it follows that
||\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}||_{2}
is indeed a bounded function, and so
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta,\tau}}\big[||\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}||_{2}^{2}\big]
exists. As for the continuity of
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta,\tau}}\big[||\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}||_{2}^{2}\big]
on a neighborhood of
\theta^{\ast}
, this follows from an application of the dominated convergence theorem and the definition of regularity in Appendix A of
[
Newey, 1990
]
, which ensures that
\mathcal{J}^{\theta,\tau}(z)
is continuous for every
\theta
and at almost all
z
.

Third, we need to prove that, if if
\mathbf{M}^{\text{sub},\tau}
is a regular parametric submodel with nuisance parameter
\rho
, and
\theta^{\ast}=(\pi^{\ast},\rho^{\ast})
is such that
\mathcal{J}^{\theta^{\ast},\tau}=\mathcal{J}^{\pi^{\ast},\mathbf{p},\tau}
, then
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta^{\ast},\tau}}\big[\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}S_{\pi^{\ast}}^{\prime}\big]=I_{m\times m}
, and
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta^{\ast},\tau}}\big[\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\prime}\big]
is a matrix of all zeros. Towards that end, first observe that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta^{\ast},\tau}}\big[\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}S_{\pi^{\ast}}^{\prime}\big]

\displaystyle=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\big[\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(X,-1,1)s_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)^{\prime}\big]

\displaystyle=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\bigg[\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)s_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\bigg[\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)s_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\textup{\text{Cov}}_{\pi^{\ast}}\bigg[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X),s_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}A_{s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))

\displaystyle=I_{m\times m},

where the fifth and sixth lines are due to Lemma (
2.2
). Also observe that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta^{\ast},\tau}}\big[\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\prime}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta^{\ast},\tau}}\bigg[\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta^{\ast},\tau}}\Bigg[D\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(X,-1,1)S_{\rho^{\ast}}(X,-1,1)^{\prime}+(1-D)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{Y=j\}}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(X,j,0)S_{\rho^{\ast}}(X,j,0)^{\prime}\Bigg].

Note that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta^{\ast},\tau}}\Bigg[D\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(X,-1,1)S_{\rho^{\ast}}(X,-1,1)^{\prime}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\Bigg[\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\frac{p_{j}(X)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)}\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}^{\prime}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}\Bigg[\frac{p_{j}(X)}{p_{\pi^{\ast}}(X)}\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}^{\prime}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{j}\Bigg[\ \big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\ast}}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}^{\prime}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{j}\Bigg[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}^{\prime}\Bigg]

and that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta^{\ast},\tau}}\Bigg[(1-D)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{Y=j\}}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(X,j,0)S_{\rho^{\ast}}(X,j,0)^{\prime}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=(1-\tau)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\textup{\text{tr}}}\mathbb{E}_{j}\Bigg[\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(X,j,0)S_{\rho^{\ast}}(X,j,0)^{\prime}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{j}\Bigg[\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)S_{\rho^{\ast}}(X,j,0)^{\prime}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{j}\Bigg[\big(s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)-\mathbb{E}_{j}[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}]\big)\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}^{\prime}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}(\gamma^{\ast}(\tau))^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\pi_{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{j}\Bigg[s_{\gamma^{\ast}(\tau)}(X)\frac{\delta}{\delta{\rho}}\big\{\log q_{j}^{\rho}(X)\big\}\big\lvert_{\rho=\rho^{\ast}}^{\prime}\Bigg].

Thus, we have that

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta^{\ast},\tau}}\Bigg[D\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(X,-1,1)S_{\rho^{\ast}}(X,-1,1)^{\prime}\Bigg]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta^{\ast},\tau}}\Bigg[(1-D)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbbold{1}{\{Y=j\}}\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}(X,j,0)S_{\rho^{\ast}}(X,j,0)^{\prime}\Bigg],

and so it must be that
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{J}^{\theta^{\ast},\tau}}\big[\psi_{\tau}^{\textup{eff}}S_{\rho^{\ast}}^{\prime}\big]
is a matrix of all zeros. Thus, we have shown that all the conditions in Theorem 2.2 in
[
Newey, 1990
]
are satisfied, and so
\widehat{\pi}_{n}
is indeed regular.

∎
